  credits must be included in the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the cancellation request is made by a registered
    creator, the report that they submit under section 122; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the cancellation request is made by a primary
    supplier who is not a registered creator, the report that they
    submit under section 127.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Credit-creation report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the annual credit-creation report that a
  registered creator submits under section 120 respecting the creation
  of provisional compliance credits during a compliance period, the
  registered creator must subtract the following from those credits:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the compliance credits created from the production
    in Canada or import into Canada of a low-carbon-intensity fuel under
    section 19 or 20 that is subsequently exported; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of compliance credits determined in
    accordance with subsection 88(2) or 90(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-submission of report

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** A registered creator
must, within 60 days after the day on which they learn of an error made
in a report that they submitted under these Regulations, other than a
report submitted under section 121, if the error exceeds the
significance threshold provided in the Methods for Verification and
Certification, submit to the Minister

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an updated report in which the error has been
  corrected; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an updated verification report that contains a
  verification statement with respect to the corrected version of the
  report.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of error

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** A registered creator
must, within five days after the day on which they learn of an error
made in a report that they submitted under subsection 120(1) or 122(1)
that resulted in the deposit of a number of compliance credits into one
of their accounts opened under section 28 that is greater than the
number that should have been deposited, send a notice of that error to
the Minister that indicates

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the report that contains the error and
  the date on which it was submitted;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of Schedule 11 or 13 to which the
  error relates and the nature of the error;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the difference between the number of compliance
  credits that were deposited and the number that should have been
  deposited; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether the account into which the compliance credits
  were deposited is a liquid-fuel-compliance-credit account opened under
  paragraph 28(a) or a gaseous-fuel-compliance-credit account opened
  under paragraph 28(b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of excess compliance credits

- **[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if the
  Minister has reason to believe, following the submission of a report
  by a registered creator or primary supplier under section 120,
  subsection 121(3) or sections 122 or 127, or following the submission
  of the form referred to in section 171, that excess compliance credits
  have been created, the Minister may suspend the excess compliance
  credits that are in any of the accounts of the registered creator or
  primary supplier.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Export

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister has reason to believe, following the
  submission of a report by a registered creator under section 120 or
  122 or a primary supplier under section 127, that excess compliance
  credits have been created by the production in Canada or the import
  into Canada of a low-carbon-intensity fuel that is subsequently
  exported, the Minister may suspend the excess compliance credits that
  are in the account of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the primary supplier or registered creator who
    exported the fuel, in the case where it was exported by a primary
    supplier or registered creator;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the primary supplier or registered creator who
    acquired the fuel from the registered creator who created the
    compliance credits, in the case where it was the subject of a
    transfer request referred to in section 108 and was exported by a
    person who is not a primary supplier or registered creator; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the registered creator who created the compliance
    credits as provisional compliance credits, in any other case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of equivalent compliance
  credits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If any number of the excess compliance credits are
  not in the accounts referred to in subsections (1) and (2), the
  Minister may suspend the same number of equivalent compliance credits
  that are in the accounts or subsequently deposited into the accounts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of suspension

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When the Minister suspends, under subsection (1) or
  (2), excess compliance credits that are in an account or suspends, for
  the first time, equivalent compliance credits under subsection (3),
  the Minister must send a notice to the account holder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The notice must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the report in which the Minister has reason to
    believe that an error exists;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the provisions of Schedule 11, 12,
    13 or 18, or the provisions of the form referred to in section 171,
    to which the error relates as well as the nature of the error;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the account into which the excess
    compliance credits were deposited;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the number of excess compliance
    credits that are to be cancelled;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the number of compliance credits,
    if any, that are suspended under subsection (1) or (2);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the number of equivalent
    compliance credits, if any, that are suspended under subsection (3)
    and the compliance period in which they were created;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an indication of whether the suspended compliance
    credits are in a liquid-fuel-compliance-credit account opened under
    paragraph 28(a) or a gaseous-fuel-compliance-credit account opened
    under paragraph 28(b); and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a statement that the Minister will suspend any
    additional equivalent compliance credits until the number of
    compliance credits that are suspended is equal to the number of
    excess compliance credits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No use or transfer

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Beginning on the day on which the notice referred to
  in subsection (4) is received by the account holder and ending on the
  day on which the suspension is lifted, the account holder must not use
  suspended compliance credits to comply with the total reduction
  requirement or a volumetric requirement set out in subsection 6(1) or
  7(1) and must not transfer suspended compliance credits under section
  106 or 112.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lifting of suspension

**[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** If an additional review
by the Minister confirms that excess compliance credits were not
created, the Minister must lift the suspension of the compliance
credits.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of excess credits

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the following circumstances, the Minister must cancel the excess
  compliance credits, or the equivalent number of compliance credits,
  that are in an account referred to in subsection 158(1) or (2):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person makes a request referred to in subsection
    155(1) for the cancellation of compliance credits;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a registered creator sends a notice to the Minister
    in accordance with section 157; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} after the Minister has suspended compliance credits
    under subsection 158(1) or (2), an additional review by the Minister
    confirms that excess compliance credits were created.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insufficient number of equivalent
  credits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the number of excess compliance credits or
  equivalent compliance credits that are to be cancelled under
  subsection (1) is greater than the number of such compliance credits
  in the account, the Minister must send a notice to the account holder
  that indicates the number of compliance credits that are missing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to balance credits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The account holder must, no later than 90 days after
  the day on which they receive the notice,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the number of equivalent compliance
    credits in the account is equal to the number of compliance credits
    that are missing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} request that the Minister cancel the equivalent
    compliance credits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of missing compliance
  credits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the request referred to in paragraph
  (3)(b), the Minister must cancel the equivalent compliance credits.

## [Measurement, Electronic Reporting and Records]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1360667 .Part}

### [Measurement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1360668 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  a person who is required by these Regulations to record any volume or
  quantity must determine that volume or quantity

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by using one or more measurement devices that meet
    the requirements of the [Weights and Measures Act](/eng/acts/W-6)
    and the regulations made under that Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with a measurement standard or method
    that is appropriate for the determination and cited in the Manual of
    Petroleum Measurement Standards, published by the American Petroleum
    Institute; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with any applicable
    emission-reduction quantification method established under
    subsection 31(1) or 32(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is no measurement device, standard or method
  referred to in subsection (1) that would allow the person to determine
  the volume or quantity in accordance with that subsection, the person
  must record the volume or quantity as accurately determined by another
  person who is independent of them and record the following information
  obtained from the other person:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their name, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the volume, expressed in cubic metres, or the
    quantity, expressed in kilograms for solids, in cubic metres for
    liquid, in cubic metres or kilograms for gas and in kilowatt-hours
    for electricity, and an indication of the fuel in question;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date on which and the location where the
    determination was made; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the measurement device, standard or method used to
    determine the volume or quantity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Volumetric correction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise specified by a provision of these
  Regulations, a person who determines a volume in accordance with
  subsection (1) must correct the volume to standard conditions.
  However, a person who imports into Canada a volume of fuel may correct
  its volume to a temperature of 15.6°C (59°F), if the person records
  the correction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Biogas energy density

- **[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  measurement of the energy density of biogas may be carried out in
  accordance with the fuel heat content monitoring requirements set out
  in section 2.D.3 of the document entitled Canada's Greenhouse Gas
  Quantification Requirements / Greenhouse Gas Reporting Program,
  published by the Minister, and must be corrected to standard
  conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum sampling

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A primary supplier must conduct the sampling of
  biogas at least once every month.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of energy density

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The weighted average of the energy density of biogas
  for a compliance period must be determined based on the measurement of
  energy density weighted by the volume of biogas produced.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rounding

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise specified by a
  provision of these Regulations, a person who performs a calculation or
  submits a report under these Regulations must round the result of the
  calculation or all values in the report, as the case may be, in
  accordance with the rounding procedures set out in International
  Standard ASTM E29-22, entitled Standard Practice for Using Significant
  Digits in Test Data to Determine Conformance with Specifications,
  published by ASTM International.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tonnes of CO~2~e

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A primary supplier must round the result of a
  calculation made under section 9 to the nearest whole tonne of CO~2~e
  or, if the result is halfway between two consecutive whole numbers, to
  the greater of those whole numbers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approved carbon intensity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A registered creator, carbon-intensity contributor or
  foreign supplier must round a carbon intensity approved by the
  Minister under subsection 85(1) to the nearest whole number or, if the
  result is halfway between two consecutive whole numbers, to the
  greater of those whole numbers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance credits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A number of compliance credits created under a
  provision of these Regulations must be rounded to the nearest whole
  number or, if the result is halfway between two consecutive whole
  numbers, to the greater of those whole numbers.

### [Electronic Reporting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1360699 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic submission --- report or
notice

- **[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who is required by these Regulations to submit a report or send
  a notice to the Minister must do so electronically in the form
  specified by the Minister and the report or notice must bear the
  signature of the person's authorized agent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paper report or notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister has not specified an electronic form
  or it is impractical for the person to submit the report or send the
  notice electronically because of circumstances beyond their control,
  the person must submit or send a paper version of the report or
  notice, signed by their authorized agent, in the form specified by the
  Minister or, if no form has been specified, in any reasonable form.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation of carbon intensity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who submits a calculation of a carbon
  intensity to the Minister must submit it electronically in the form
  specified by the Minister or, if no form has been specified, in any
  reasonable form.

### [Recording and Retention of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1360707 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When records are made

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided in these Regulations, any person who is required to record
information must record it within 30 days after the day on which it
becomes available.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention of information

- **[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who is required by these Regulations to record information or
  keep it up to date, submit a report or plan or send a notice must
  retain a record of the information or copy of the report, plan or
  notice, as the case may be, as well as any supporting documents, for a
  period of 10 years after the day on which the information is recorded
  or updated, the report or plan is submitted or the notice is sent, as
  the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification or certification body

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A verification body or certification body must, in
  accordance with the Methods for Verification and Certification, retain
  the books and records that they have verified or certified, or a copy
  of those books and records, for a period of 10 years after the day on
  which they are verified or certified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission-reduction projects

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who is required to retain any information
  and documents, including reports, plans, notices and supporting
  documents, that relate to a CO~2~e-emission-reduction project referred
  to in paragraph 19(1)(a) or 20(a) must retain the information and
  documents for a period of 10 years after the day on which the carrying
  out of the project ceases to create compliance credits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location of records

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A foreign supplier, primary supplier,
  carbon-intensity contributor or registered creator who is required
  under subsection (1) or (3) to retain any information or documents,
  including supporting documents, must keep the information and
  documents at their principal place of business in Canada or at another
  place in Canada where they may be inspected, in which case the foreign
  supplier, primary supplier, carbon-intensity contributor or registered
  creator must provide the Minister with the civic address of that other
  place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (4), a registered creator who
  carries out a CO~2~e-emission-reduction project outside Canada or a
  foreign supplier or carbon-intensity contributor who is outside Canada
  may keep the information and documents referred to in that subsection
  at their principal place of business outside Canada, in which case
  they must provide the Minister with the civic address of that place.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records related to compliance units

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  primary supplier who, on December 31, 2022, is required by section 38
  of the [Renewable Fuels Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189) to
  keep a record, a copy of a report or notice or a supporting document
  that is related to a gasoline compliance unit referred to in
  subsection 169(1) of these Regulations or a distillate compliance unit
  referred to in subsection 170(1) of these Regulations must keep those
  documents until March 31, 2033.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other records

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A primary supplier who, on December 31, 2023, is
  required by section 38 of the [Renewable Fuels
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189) to keep a record, a copy
  of a report or notice or a supporting document, other than one
  referred to in subsection (1), must keep those documents for 10 years
  after the day on which they make the record or submit or send the
  report or notice, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location of records

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A primary supplier who is required under
  subsection (1) or (2) to keep any records, copies or supporting
  documents must keep them at their principal place of business in
  Canada, or at another place in Canada where they may be inspected, in
  which case the primary supplier must provide the Minister with the
  civic address of that other place.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information requested by Minister

**[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
required to record any information must, on the Minister's request,
provide a copy of the record to the Minister.

## [Transitional Provisions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1360730 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gasoline compliance units

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  on April 30, 2024, a primary supplier owns gasoline compliance units
  under the [Renewable Fuels
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189), the number of compliance
  credits that is determined by the following formula must be deposited
  into their account that was opened under paragraph 28(a) of these
  Regulations:

  ::: {#1360734 .Subsection}
  CI~diff~ × (GCU × D) × 10^-9^

  where

  [CI~diff~]{.dfn}
  :   is the difference between 59 g/MJ and the reference carbon
      intensity for the liquid class, as set out in item 1, column 2, of
      Schedule 1 to these Regulations;

  [GCU]{.dfn}
  :   is the number of gasoline compliance units that the primary
      supplier owned at the end of the trading period established by the
      [Renewable Fuels Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189) for
      the 2022 compliance period; and

  [D]{.dfn}
  :   is 23 419 MJ/m^3^.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Volumetric requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 12(1), each compliance
  credit deposited under subsection (1) is deemed to have been created
  under paragraph 19(1)(b) or (c) by producing in Canada or importing
  into Canada an equivalent volume of low-carbon-intensity fuel that is
  ethanol.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distillate compliance units

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  on April 30, 2024, a primary supplier owns distillate compliance units
  under the [Renewable Fuels
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189), the number of compliance
  credits that is determined by the following formula must be deposited
  into their account that was opened under paragraph 28(a) of these
  Regulations:

  ::: {#1360744 .Subsection}
  CI~diff~ × (DCU × D) × 10^-9^

  where

  [CI~diff~]{.dfn}
  :   is the difference between 35 g/MJ and the reference carbon
      intensity for the liquid class, as set out in item 1, column 2, of
      Schedule 1 to these Regulations;

  [DCU]{.dfn}
  :   is the number of distillate compliance units that the primary
      supplier owned at the end of the trading period established by the
      [Renewable Fuels Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189) for
      the 2022 compliance period; and

  [D]{.dfn}
  :   is 35 057 MJ/m^3^.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Volumetric requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection12(2), each compliance
  credit deposited under subsection (1) is deemed to have been created
  under paragraph 19(1)(b) or (c) by producing in Canada or importing
  into Canada an equivalent volume of a diesel replacement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for deposit of credits

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** A primary supplier may
request the deposit of compliance credits into their account in
accordance with section 169 or 170 of these Regulations by providing a
form to the Minister, no later than April 30, 2024, that is signed by
their authorized agent and contains the following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of gasoline compliance units that the
  primary supplier owned at the end of the trading period established by
  the [Renewable Fuels Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189) for
  the 2022 compliance period;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of distillate compliance units that the
  primary supplier owned at the end of the trading period established by
  the [Renewable Fuels Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2010-189) for
  the 2022 compliance period; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the number of compliance credits that will be
  deposited into their account that was opened under paragraph 28(a) of
  these Regulations.

## [SCHEDULE 3]{.scheduleLabel}[(Paragraph 1(4)(o) and subsections 10(1) and (3) and 25(1) and section 26)]{.OriginatingRef}[Contents of Registration Report]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1360793 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** The following information with respect to the primary supplier
  or registered creator, as the case may be:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their name, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} their business number, if any;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name, title, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address of their authorized
    agent; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name, title, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address of a contact person,
    unless the contact person is the authorized agent.

- **2** The following information with respect to each facility at which
  the primary supplier produces gasoline or diesel:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of the facility; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether it is gasoline or diesel that is produced
    at the facility.

- **3** The following information with respect to each province into
  which the primary supplier imports gasoline or diesel into Canada from
  outside Canada:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the province; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether it is gasoline or diesel that is imported
    into the province by the primary supplier.

- **4** If the registered creator intends to create compliance credits
  by carrying out a CO~2~e-emission-reduction project referred to in
  section 30 of these Regulations, the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of the facility where the project
    is carried out or, if the project is carried out at a location other
    than a facility, the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of the equipment that is used to
    carry out the project;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type of any fuel, material input or energy
    source that is used to carry out the project if an application for
    the approval of the carbon intensity of that fuel, material input or
    energy source is required by the applicable emission-reduction
    quantification method established under subsection 31(1) or 32(1) of
    these Regulations; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the project is one described in paragraph 30(d)
    of these Regulations, for each co-processed low-carbon intensity
    fuel that is imported into Canada for use as a fuel in Canada,
    whether as a neat fuel or as part of a blend, its type and the name
    of the province into which the co-processed low carbon intensity
    fuel is imported.

- **5** If the registered creator intends to create compliance credits
  by importing into Canada a low-carbon-intensity fuel for use in Canada
  as a fuel, whether as neat fuel or as part of a blend, the following
  information for each province into which the registered creator
  intends to import fuel:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the province; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type of fuel that will be imported.

- **6** If the registered creator intends to create compliance credits
  by producing a low-carbon-intensity fuel for use in Canada as a fuel,
  whether as neat fuel or as part of a blend, the following information
  with respect to each facility at which the fuel will be produced:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of the facility; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type of fuel produced at the facility.

- **7** If the registered creator intends to create compliance credits
  by producing biogas for use in equipment to produce electricity, the
  following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of each facility at which the
    biogas will be produced; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of each facility at which the
    electricity will be produced.

- **8** If the registered creator intends to create compliance credits
  by displacing the use in Canada of a quantity of fuel in the liquid
  class as a fuel for a vehicle with the use in Canada of a quantity of
  propane, renewable propane, co-processed low-carbon-intensity propane,
  compressed natural gas, compressed renewable natural gas, liquefied
  natural gas or liquefied renewable natural gas as a fuel for a
  vehicle, the following information with respect to each fuelling
  station that supplied the fuel that displaces the fuel in the liquid
  class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of the fuelling station; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type of each fuel that will be supplied at the
    fuelling station for which the registered creator intends to create
    compliance credits.

- **9** If the registered creator is a charging-network operator and
  intends to create compliance credits by displacing the use in Canada
  of a quantity of fuel in the liquid class as a fuel for a vehicle with
  the use in Canada of electricity as an energy source supplied to an
  electric vehicle by a charging station that is intended primarily for
  use by the occupants of a private dwelling-place, with respect to each
  charging station at which the electricity will be supplied, the name
  of the province in which it is located.

- **10** If the registered creator is a charging-network operator and
  intends to create compliance credits by displacing the use in Canada
  of a quantity of fuel in the liquid class as a fuel for a vehicle with
  the use in Canada of electricity as an energy source supplied to an
  electric vehicle by a charging station that is intended primarily for
  use by the public, with respect to each charging station at which the
  electricity will be supplied, the name of the province in which it is
  located.

- **11** If the registered creator is a charging-site host and intends
  to create compliance credits by displacing the use in Canada of a
  quantity of fuel in the liquid class as a fuel for a vehicle with the
  use in Canada of electricity as an energy source supplied to an
  electric vehicle by a charging station other than a charging station
  described in sections 9 and 10 of this Schedule, with respect to each
  charging station at which the electricity will be supplied, the name
  of the province in which it is located.

- **12** If the registered creator intends to create compliance credits
  by displacing the use in Canada of a quantity of fuel in the liquid
  class as fuel for a vehicle with the use in Canada of a quantity of
  hydrogen as an energy source for a hydrogen fuel cell vehicle, the
  name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal place and, if any, civic
  address of each hydrogen fuelling station at which the hydrogen will
  be supplied.
::::

::::: section
:::: {#1360826 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE 4]{.scheduleLabel}[(Paragraphs 34(2)(a), 37(2)(a) and (c), 38(2)(a) and 40(2)(a) and (c))]{.OriginatingRef}[Contents of Application for Recognition of CO~2~e-Emission-Reduction Project]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1360827 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** The following information with respect to the applicant:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their name, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name, title, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address of the authorized agent;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name, title, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address of a contact person,
    unless the contact person is the authorized agent.

- **2** A description of the project that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, GPS coordinates to the fifth decimal
    place and, if any, civic address of any facility at which the
    project is to be carried out;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name, serial number, GPS coordinates to the
    fifth decimal place and, if any, civic address of any equipment with
    which the project is to be carried out;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the project and the anticipated
    start- and end dates for carrying out the project;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the information required by the emission-reduction
    quantification method established under subsection 31(1) or 32(1),
    whichever is applicable to the project, including an explanation of
    how the project is anticipated to lower the carbon intensity of a
    fuel in the liquid or gaseous class and any supporting documents;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an application for the recognition
    of a CO~2~e-emission-reduction project using a generic
    emission-reduction quantification method established under
    subsection 31(1) of these Regulations,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the specified activities that would have been
      carried out if not for the implementation of the project and the
      CO~2~e emissions that would have resulted from those activities,
      as well as the greenhouse gas sources and sinks that have been
      selected by the applicant to determine those CO~2~e emissions and
      a rationale for the selection of those greenhouse gas sources and
      sinks;

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the greenhouse gas sources and sinks that have
      been selected by the applicant and are relevant to the project and
      a rationale for the selection of those greenhouse gas sources and
      sinks;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the annual reduction in the quantity of CO~2~e that
    is anticipated to result from the project, expressed in tonnes of
    CO~2~e, including any relevant data and calculations and any
    technical documents that are used in support of those calculations;
    and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the regulatory regime and the
    federal or provincial laws under which the project operates and any
    federal or provincial laws and programs under which it receives
    funding.
::::
:::::

::::: section
:::: {#1360843 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE 5]{.scheduleLabel}[(Paragraphs 62(2)(c) and (d))]{.OriginatingRef}[Contents of Certification Scheme Operation Report]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1360844 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** The following information with respect to the person who is the
  scheme owner:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their name, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name, title, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address of the authorized agent;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name, title, civic address, postal address,
    telephone number and, if any, email address of a contact person,
    unless the contact person is the authorized agent.

- **2** The following information regarding the members of the
  certification scheme:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of current members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of new members;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the number of suspended members; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the number of members whose participation in the
    scheme has been terminated, as well as the reasons for the
    termination.

- **3** The following information regarding the operation of the
  certification scheme:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of years remaining in the approval
    period of the scheme;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the changes made to the scope of the scheme;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of certified feedstock, by type and
    country of origin;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the number of audits conducted, by members of the
    scheme and by type of feedstock;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the number of situations of non-conformity
    detected, by members of the scheme;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the number of situations of non-conformity
    detected, by type and degree of severity; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the number of cases of fraud or irregularities
    detected.

- **4** The following information regarding the design and operation of
  the management system of the certification scheme:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of stakeholder involvement during the
    drafting and reviewing of the scheme as well as the responses given
    to any contributions by stakeholders;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the criteria for the recognition and accreditation
    of certification bodies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the qualification requirements for auditors,
    according to their role;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the impartiality requirements for certification
    bodies and auditors;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the procedures for detecting and addressing
    non-conformity;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the procedures for monitoring the certification
    bodies;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the procedures for preventing fraud, including
    detection and resolution, and follow-up procedures for suspected
    fraud and other irregularities;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a description of any complaints lodged against the
    certification scheme and the manner in which they were resolved; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an analysis of the scheme's requirements, compared
    to industry's actual best practices.

- **5** A description of the procedures that permit an assessment of the
  availability of the following documents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a translation of the certification scheme's
    requirements and procedures in the common language of the country or
    region from which any certified feedstock originates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a list of certified feedstock producers and their
    corresponding certificates; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any auditor reports.

- **6** A description of any improvements made by the scheme owner to
  the certification scheme and, if the scheme owner has any
  recommendations regarding any matter related to the regulatory regime,
  those recommendations.

## [SCHEDULE 6]{.scheduleLabel}[(Paragraphs 75(1)(a) and (b), (6)(a) and (7)(a) and 96(4)(a))]{.OriginatingRef}[Default Carbon Intensity]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1360879 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** The default carbon intensity is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 80 gCO~2~e/MJ for the years 2022 and 2023;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 79 gCO~2~e/MJ for the year 2024;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 78 gCO~2~e/MJ for the year 2025;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 77 gCO~2~e/MJ for the year 2026;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 76 gCO~2~e/MJ for the year 2027;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 74 gCO~2~e/MJ for the year 2028;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} 73 gCO~2~e/MJ for the year 2029; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} 72 gCO~2~e/MJ for the year 2030 and the following
    years.

- **2** The quantity of CO~2~e that is associated with the extraction or
  production, as the case may be, of a feedstock is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel}  0 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input produced
    from a feedstock that is derived from one of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} forest biomass derived from fire prevention and
      protection activities or from clearing activities that are not
      related to harvesting, such as infrastructure installation, pest
      and disease control and road maintenance,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} crop residues or damaged crops,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} secondary forest residues that are by-products
      of industrial wood-processing operations,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} used or inedible organics from a residential
      area, a retail store, a restaurant, a caterer or a food processing
      plant,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} used fat and used vegetable oils,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} used animal litter,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} animal materials, including manure,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} industrial effluents,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} municipal wastewater, and

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} used construction materials and demolition
      materials,

    - [(xi)]{.lawlabel} residues that are directly generated by
      agriculture, aquaculture and fisheries, but not residues from
      related industries or from processing,

    - [(xii)]{.lawlabel} water,

    - [(xiii)]{.lawlabel} waste from a waste processing facility that
      produces biogas, and

    - [(xiv)]{.lawlabel} carbon dioxide that has been captured from the
      atmosphere;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 15 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input produced
    from a feedstock derived from crops grown solely for energy
    production and that are not traditionally grown for food and feed
    purposes;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 20 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input produced
    from a feedstock that is an oil extracted from oilseed crops; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 35 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input produced
    from any other feedstock.

- **3** The quantity of CO~2~e that is released during the production of
  the fuel or material input from the feedstock, the transportation of
  the feedstock and intermediary products used to produce the fuel or
  material input and the distribution of the fuel or material input to
  end users is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 13 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input that is
    produced at a facility that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} uses thermal energy and electricity where more
      than 50% of that energy is from non-fossil sources, electricity
      with a carbon intensity of less than 100 gCO~2~e/MJ, hydrogen from
      renewable sources, hydrogen from natural gas with carbon capture
      and storage or a mix of those sources, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} does not use liquid or solid fossil fuels in
      stationary applications;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 30 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input that is
    produced at a facility, other than one described in subparagraph
    (a)(i), that does not use liquid or solid fossil fuels in stationary
    application; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 65 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input that is
    produced at any other fuel production facility.

- **4** The quantity of CO~2~e t that is released during the compression
  or liquefaction process of the fuel material input is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 12 gCO~2~e/MJ for compressed hydrogen that is
    supplied by a hydrogen fuelling station;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 60 gCO~2~e/MJ for liquefied hydrogen that is
    supplied by a hydrogen fuelling station; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 0 gCO~2~e/MJ for all other fuels, including
    hydrogen referred to in section 95 of these Regulations that is
    supplied for use as a fuel.

- **5** The quantity of CO~2~e that is associated with the production of
  electricity used during the production of the fuel or material input
  is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 25 gCO~2~e/MJ for hydrogen produced from
    electrolysis using electricity with a carbon intensity of 50 g/MJ or
    less;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 150 gCO~2~e/MJ for any other hydrogen produced from
    electrolysis; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 0 gCO~2~e/MJ for any other fuel.

- **6** The quantity of CO~2~e that is released during the
  transportation of the feedstock and intermediary products used to
  produce the fuel or material input and the distribution of the fuel or
  material input to end users, in the case of a total transportation
  distance of no less than 1500 km, is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 0 gCO~2~e/MJ for a fuel or material input for which
    the sum of the distance between the site where the feedstock was
    extracted, harvested or produced and the facility where the fuel or
    material input was produced and the distance between that facility
    and the location of final distribution to end users is less than
    1500 km; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 4 gCO~2~e/MJ for any other fuel or material input.

- **7** The quantity of CO~2~e that is released during the combustion of
  the fuel or the use of the material input, per megajoule of energy
  produced, is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 0 gCO~2~e/MJ for hydrogen or carbon dioxide derived
    from atmospheric carbon dioxide;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 1 gCO~2~e/MJ for biogas or renewable natural gas;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 2 gCO~2~e/MJ for ethanol or a fuel derived from
    biomass other than biodiesel;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 3 gCO~2~e/MJ for biodiesel; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 72 gCO~2~e/MJ for any other fuel.

- **8** The carbon intensity of fossil fuels and energy sources is equal
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 110 gCO~2~e/MJ for compressed hydrogen;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 150 gCO~2~e/MJ for liquefied hydrogen;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 76 gCO~2~e/MJ for propane;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 68 gCO~2~e/MJ for natural gas;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 113 gCO~2~e/MJ for liquefied natural gas; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 72 gCO~2~e/MJ for compressed natural gas.

- **9** The carbon intensity of electricity for a province is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 14 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Ontario;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 5 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Quebec;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 224 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Nova Scotia;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 89 gCO~2~e/MJ  in New Brunswick;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 7 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Manitoba;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 11 gCO~2~e/MJ  in British Columbia;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} 2 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Prince Edward Island;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} 237 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Saskatchewan;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 218 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Alberta;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} 16 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Newfoundland and Labrador;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} 30 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Yukon;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} 71 gCO~2~e/MJ  in the Northwest Territories; and

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} 313 gCO~2~e/MJ  in Nunavut.
::::
